How to Build an IT Risk Register in Excel

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 15 • Originally published at circuits.fit on Nov 15

Write the scales first, then look scores up from a 5 by 5 grid. Columns, formulas, severity bands and a worked example.

Edited by Emilio Santos. October 25, 2026.

The IT or security manager who needs a working risk register in a spreadsheet, and the finance or audit lead who has to read it.

An IT risk register is only as good as the agreement behind its numbers. The spreadsheet part takes an afternoon. Getting two people to give the same risk the same score takes longer, and it is the part that matters.

This guide builds a register in Excel (it works the same way in LibreOffice Calc and Google Sheets) around a 5 by 5 likelihood by impact matrix: the columns, the lookup formulas, severity bands that set how often each risk is reviewed, and a worked example with the arithmetic shown.

A risk register lists the things that could go wrong with your technology, how likely and how damaging each one is, what you are doing about it, and who is accountable. It is a decision tool: it tells leadership where to spend money and attention, and it shows an auditor, insurer or board that risks are known and owned.

Keep it separate from two neighbouring lists. A vulnerability or findings tracker holds individual technical issues with fix dates. A risk exception register holds approved, time-limited departures from a control. A risk register sits above both: "a critical system is compromised through an unpatched internet-facing service" is a risk; a specific missing patch is a finding; an approved decision not to patch one server until March is an exception. The three can reference each other by ID, but merging them makes each harder to read.

Calculated columns follow: inherent score and severity, residual score and severity, next review due, and an overdue flag.

Write risk statements in the form "because of [cause], [event] could happen, leading to [consequence]". "Phishing" is a topic. "Because staff can approve payment changes by email, an attacker impersonating a supplier could redirect a payment, leading to a direct financial loss" is a risk someone can score and treat.

Before anyone scores anything, write a one-line definition for each level, and put them on a settings tab next to the matrix. For example:

Where you can, attach numbers to impact levels that fit your company: a dollar range, hours of downtime, number of customer records. "Major" means something very different to a 30-person firm and a 3,000-person one. If you use a published method such as NIST SP 800-30, follow its structure, but the definitions still have to be written for your organization.

The middle levels are the hardest. If Possible and Likely cannot be told apart in writing, most risks will end up scored 3 by 3, and the register will look tidy while saying very little.

Put the matrix on the settings tab as a grid: likelihood 1 to 5 down the rows, impact 1 to 5 across the columns, and the score in each cell. The default is likelihood multiplied by impact, so the grid runs from 1 in the top left to 25 in the bottom right. Say the grid occupies Settings!C12:G16.

In the register, look the score up from the grid instead of multiplying in each row:

Why bother, when =H5*I5 gives the same answer? Because many organizations eventually want to weight impact more heavily than likelihood, for example so that a rare but severe event scores higher than a frequent trivial one. With a lookup grid, that is a change to a few cells on one tab. With multiplication, it is a rewrite of every row.

Use data validation to limit likelihood and impact cells to whole numbers from 1 to 5. A typed 3.5 or "med" breaks the lookup. If you want the heat map look, apply conditional formatting to the grid itself and to the severity columns so the colour follows the band.

Turn the score into a severity with a small table of minimum scores and an approximate-match lookup:

VLOOKUP with TRUE returns the band for the largest minimum score that does not exceed the score, so the minimums must stay in ascending order. These bands are an example, not a standard. Set them to match your risk appetite, and write down who approved them.

Add a third column to the band table for review frequency, then calculate next review due from residual severity:

This is what keeps the register alive. High and Critical risks come back to the table often; Low ones do not consume meeting time.

Two invented risks, scored with the scales and bands above.

R-007: payment redirection. "Because supplier bank details can be changed on the strength of an email, an attacker impersonating a supplier could redirect a payment, leading to a direct financial loss."

R-012: ransomware on file servers. "Because staff can open email attachments on machines with access to shared drives, ransomware could encrypt the file servers, leading to a multi-day outage."

R-012 shows something useful. Good controls cut the likelihood, but the impact of a successful attack stays severe, so the residual score lands exactly on the High boundary. That is not a scoring error. It tells the owner that further work should aim at impact, for example faster restore or segmenting the shares, and it tells the board this risk deserves attention even with strong controls in place.

A 3 by 3 is easier to score consistently. A 5 by 5 gives more resolution, but only if the five levels are clearly defined. Start with whichever your team will actually apply the same way.

Enough that every significant system and process is covered, few enough that each one gets reviewed. Many small and mid-sized companies keep a few dozen. Combine near-duplicates.

An accepted risk stays on the risk register with treatment "accept" and a review date. If acceptance means departing from a specific control, record that departure in the exception register too, with an expiry.

The formulas on this page do. Functions such as MAXIFS, useful for pulling the latest review date from a review log, need Excel 2019 or later.

Company names to website domains, in bulk: a confidence-scored approach

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 15 • Originally published at cybermaxtools.com

Turning a column of company names into domains looks like a one-liner until the first search result is a directory, a parked lookalike or a different company with the same name. Here is a workflow that records evidence and a confidence for every match, with Python, JavaScript and no-code versions.

For a few names, search each one and check the homepage. For a list, use a company-name-to-domain lookup that checks evidence instead of taking the first search result: Namewhere on Apify matches each name against Wikidata, guesses and tests likely domains, opens the homepage to confirm the brand, rejects parked domains and returns the domain with a 0-1 confidence score, at $0.0008 per match (misses are free).

A real record (input "Stripe", 24 Sep 2026)

Source: Namewhere (Apify Actor cybermax/company-to-domain) README, real run on 24 Sep 2026

Search results mix the company with review sites, directories, news and lookalike domains, and many short names have a parked or unrelated .com. A lookup built for this checks that the homepage really names the brand and records why it chose the domain, so you can trust a 0.95 and review a 0.65.

Typical uses: cleaning a CRM import, matching a trade-show or grant list to websites, enriching an investor portfolio, or giving an AI agent a reliable first step before it looks anything else up.

Is there a free company name to domain API? Namewhere charges only for matches, and Apify's monthly free credit covers a first list of a few thousand names.

How accurate is it? Every row carries a 0-1 confidence and the evidence (Wikidata, name match, homepage check). Filter on confidence to trade coverage for precision.

Can it handle companies outside the US? Yes. Add a country code to each name ("Name | de") or set the country field to rank local domains first.

Can an AI agent use it? Yes. Apify's MCP server exposes it as cybermax--company-to-domain for Claude, Cursor and other MCP clients.

Try it on your own list: Namewhere on Apify (Apify's free plan includes $5 of usage a month).

Telegram curbs new in-chat apps amid a no-tap hacking scare

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ordinary bots can no longer send the interactive messages. Researchers say the reported flaws affected older Telegram apps and are fixed in current builds.

Telegram has blocked ordinary bots from sending its new interactive in-message apps, known as HTML widgets, even in private chats. A November 13 reply in its Bot API repository confirmed the restriction as researchers and Telegram disputed security flaws in the feature.

Responding to a developer's report of failed widget messages, Telegram Bot API repository contributor levlam confirmed the restriction: “Yes, currently, general bots aren't allowed to use widgets even in private chats.” The developer reported receiving the error INPUT_MEDIA_WIDGET_NOT_ALLOWED after previously sending widgets successfully.

Telegram's Bot API changelog dates the introduction of HTML-widget messaging to Bot API 10.4 on November 9. The widget guide still listed private-chat production availability on November 14, despite levlam's confirmation that general bots cannot use it. The confirmation concerns HTML widgets; it does not announce a shutdown of ordinary bot messages or all bot services.

Unlike Mini Apps, which open a web page on demand, widgets run inside the message itself. Telegram's widget documentation describes an isolated environment that runs their code on the user's device without direct internet access. Requests to the bot pass through Telegram instead.

The feature has a direct wallet connection in its documented design. Telegram's changelog describes widget queries involving a user's TON wallet card, wallet-signed data or notification of a wallet transaction. Those capabilities explain the relevance for crypto users, but do not themselves demonstrate unauthorized signing or asset theft.

As of November 14, the tg-widget-rce researchers say in their research archive that its published vulnerabilities affected listed older clients and are fixed in current Telegram builds. The archive includes Android builds, Telegram for iOS 13.2, Telegram Desktop 7.4.0 and Telegram for macOS 13.1, with different findings across versions. It does not establish a definitive minimum safe version for every client.

The researchers say widget code can execute during chat layout without a tap, but delivery requires a bot permitted to send widgets and the reported vulnerabilities depend on the client build. Their technical index distinguishes app-level code execution on selected older Android builds from results contained in an isolated widget process on others. A widget-process result does not establish control of the whole device.

OceanBase counts separate the database engine from the management consoles

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

OceanBase is a distributed database with MySQL and Oracle compatibility modes. Its public presence can include the SQL listener, a tenant management console, a developer tool, or documentation. A product title count does not identify which of those surfaces is answering.

On November 15, 2026, a ZoomEye count-only probe used sub_type=all, page 1, and page size 1. app="OceanBase" returned 0, title="OceanBase" returned 354, and http.body="OceanBase" returned 4,174. The body count is broad and likely includes documentation, release pages, and client libraries. None of the observations proves a reachable SQL service or an exposed management console.

The database uses users, system privileges, object privileges, and roles. OceanBase's documentation describes login authentication, role activation, and user privilege views. Review the SQL endpoint, the tenant, the user, the active roles, and the object grants together. A role that is granted to a user but inactive can change the effective authority after login.

OceanBase Cloud Platform and Developer Center are management planes with their own users and roles. A console user is not necessarily a database user. Record the console, the tenant manager, and the database role separately. The same display name in two systems is not evidence of the same identity.

Find the SQL port, TLS configuration, tenant name, and authentication plugin. Confirm that a direct connection uses the intended network path and that the management console is not reachable through the same public hostname. Check whether a database user can see a tenant or object outside its assigned environment.

For self-hosted deployments, inspect the backup path and the cluster metadata store. A distributed database depends on services that may not be visible in a product-title search. Inventory those dependencies with the database owner rather than guessing from a page banner.

Create a user with a narrow role, activate it, and confirm that an object outside its grant is denied. Repeat after a role change and after reconnecting, because OceanBase documents that some privilege changes take effect after reconnection. Verify that an administrator cannot accidentally use a stale session to retain a removed privilege.

Log the tenant, user, active role, statement class, and result. Do not log credentials or full queries that contain sensitive values.

For each candidate, identify whether the page is a database endpoint, a management console, a documentation site, or a client project. Record the service owner and the exact evidence. A zero application fingerprint is not a zero-deployment finding for a database that may not expose a web title.

The security question is who can connect, which tenant they reach, and which role is active. Start with that question rather than the product count.

$3.4 Billion — Anthropic Order for Gas Power Plants!

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Wow, this is a nasty, dirty, polluting stunner I didn’t see until just now.

Anthropic, the AI company behind Claude, has reportedly signed a $3.4 billion contract to buy gas-fired engines (power plants) from OnePWR Solutions, a Texas-based energy company. These gas power plants would be able to provide Anthropic’s AI data centers with an enormous amount of off-grid electricity — with the real cost being an enormous amount of air pollution and global warming emissions.

If you thought Anthropic was supposed to be “the good guy” in the AI industry, perhaps think again.

The Financial Times, which broke the news on this, says that this is the “largest known single purchase of power-generation equipment by an AI lab.” It’s this line from Futurism that really sums up my feelings: “It isn’t just symptomatic of a desperate race to secure the necessary utilities: it’s also one of the clearest signs yet that the AI industry has turned its back on the growing climate crisis in favor of pollution-spewing gas generators it’s installing across the globe.” Indeed. It’s one thing to be racing to be a leader in this field, but to turn to polluting, climate-destroying gas power plants instead of solar and wind power, that’s absolutely disappointing and — frankly — morally disgusting.

For the heck of it, to see what this company’s AI bot thought about the power choice it made, I decided to ask Claude “Are gas power plants harmful to our climate?” The response was as follows:

“Yes, though they’re less harmful than coal and more harmful than most alternatives.

“Bottom line: Gas is a cleaner fossil fuel, not a clean one. Its climate impact is best judged by what it replaces (coal vs. renewables) and by how tightly methane leaks are controlled. Carbon capture could reduce plant emissions, but it’s still costly and not widely deployed.”

So, yeah, when you’re choosing to buy $3.4 billion worth of gas power plants instead of renewable energy power plants, that is without a doubt going to be a horrible decision for our climate. I’m sure if I asked Claude about the risk humans race from climate change, the answer would not be uplifting. But I’m not going to go there. Instead, I’ll turn back to Futurism for this summary: “Researchers have found that the effects of AI on the environment are nothing short of devastating. And the prognosis for the future is grim. Last month, environmental nonprofit Sierra Club estimated that upcoming AI data centers could release 2.1 billion metric tons of carbon dioxide by 2030, wiping out nearly 15 years worth of greenhouse gas reduction efforts.”

Indeed — we’re facing drastic increases in emissions from the very dirty AI industry. The good news is people are already showing they’re fed up with AI data centers. Apparently, out of 41 ballot measures related to data centers that were up for a vote in the recent midterm elections, only one (1) went the way of the AI industry. That said, I don’t see investments in growth stopping right now, as is evidenced by this $3.4 billion gas engine order and OpenAI and others burning through billions of dollars a quarter.

8 Neo-Western Shows That Define The Genre

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The western was one of the first TV genres to gain traction. Like cop shows and variety shows and classic situation comedies, the western is an enduring staple of the television landscape, and like those other genres, it never goes away; it just evolves. Back in the TV western’s heyday, old-school cowboy shows like Lancer and Wagon Train and The Virginian dominated the airwaves. But audiences eventually fell out of love with their tired tropes and rigid formulas and romanticized view of the ugliest period in American history, and the genre had to adapt with the times.

Now, the airwaves (or, rather, streaming-waves) are dominated by neo-westerns, subverting the conventions of those old classics. Neo-westerns can either bring the tropes of westerns into a modern-day setting, like Yellowstone and Justified, or they can do something else entirely, like the spacefaring adventures of Cowboy Bebop or the philosophical meditations of HBO’s Westworld.

Taylor Sheridan has singlehandedly revitalized the TV western with the Yellowstone saga. Yellowstone has proven to be so popular that it’s no longer just a hit show in its own right; it’s been expanded into a Marvel-sized cinematic universe of sequels, prequels, and spinoffs. We’ve seen different eras of the Dutton family, different corners of the Yellowstone universe, and different chapters of the overall timeline.

But the flagship show remains the most compelling. Yellowstone brings the land disputes of Shane and the complicated family dynamics of East of Eden and the horse-wrangling antics of The Cowboys into a modern setting. The show relaunched Kevin Costner’s career and made Sheridan the Shonda Rhimes of dad TV.

Walker, Texas Ranger ran for the better part of a decade, and its success had nothing to do with the subtlety of its storytelling or the profundity of its themes or the poignancy of its character development; it had everything to do with the irresistible star power of Chuck Norris. Norris was one of the greatest action heroes to take to the screen, and Walker, Texas Ranger was the perfect vehicle for his talents.

Viewers spent nine seasons tuning in to watch Norris’ U.S. Marine-turned-Texas Ranger/kung fu teacher Cordell Walker use his martial arts skills to bring bad guys to justice. Walker, Texas Ranger was the last classic TV western before Deadwood and Hell on Wheels would bring a gritty realism to the genre, and it ran for more than 200 episodes.

Kurt Sutter’s creation of Sons of Anarchy was primarily influenced by Hamlet. It reimagines all the archetypes from William Shakespeare’s seminal play — the grieving son, the usurping stepdad, the morally gray mother — and filtered them through the lens of a contemporary California biker gang. Sons of Anarchy is essentially The Sopranos on wheels. It has all the crime family dynamics of The Sopranos, contrasting relatable family situations with life-or-death stakes, but with the rubber-burning thrills of hitting the open road atop a Harley.

While you could accurately describe it as Hamlet meets The Sopranos on wheels, Sons of Anarchy’s setting is very western-coded. Jax Teller and his fellow bikers are modern-day cowboys — on a steel horse, they ride — and all the chases along dusty roads and armed standoffs in the desert feel very analogous to classic TV westerns.

Michael Crichton’s original Westworld film was a pretty straightforward chase movie. The theme park full of robotic cowboys was just a novel setting for a relentless cat-and-mouse pursuit between Yul Brynner’s unstoppable gunslinger and Richard Benjamin’s frightened tourist. But Jonathan Nolan and Lisa Joy took the bones of that old B-movie and turned it into a complex, thought-provoking philosophical examination of artificial intelligence for HBO.

Westworld was poised to be HBO’s sci-fi alternative to Game of Thrones. Where Thrones had branched outside fantasy fans to attract mainstream viewers, Westworld hoped to do the same. Ultimately, it was much too complicated to have that kind of impact. But even just as a western fan, it was fun to see such a grand, cinematic show deconstruct the genre in such an experimental and esoteric way on such a huge, ambitious scale.

Adapted from Tony Hillerman’s Leaphorn & Chee books, Dark Winds essentially brings a neo-western edge to an old-school cop show. It combines elements of westerns, police noirs, and psychological thrillers in a chilling tale of three Navajo Tribal Police officers patrolling the Four Corners area in the American Southwest, circa the ‘70s.

It mixes the frontier justice of a western like Gunslinger with the mystery and intrigue of a neo-noir like Chinatown. Zahn McClarnon shines in the lead role of grizzled cop Joe Leaphorn, and his uneasy alliance with a border patrol agent and an undercover federal officer provides a compelling dramatic anchor for the series.

Cowboy Bebop was the gateway anime that opened a lot of western viewers’ eyes to the joys of Japanese animation in the late ‘90s. This sci-fi western noir takes all the familiar tropes of an old cowboy show — bounty hunters chasing bad guys, drifting from town to town, fighting crime in a lawless wasteland — and launches them to the edge of the universe.

In a future where the Solar System has been colonized, and the human race is more divided than ever, the crew of the Bebop balance their fledgling bounty-hunting career with their heartbreaking personal struggles. Every episode is a dazzling intergalactic adventure, but it’s also a deeply touching human drama about fascinating, layered, sympathetic characters. Cowboy Bebop paved the way for every TV space western from Firefly to The Mandalorian.

After playing a hardware salesman-turned-reluctant sheriff in HBO’s Deadwood, Timothy Olyphant took his gun belt and his cowboy boots and swaggered 130-ish years into the future to play a very different kind of TV gunslinger in Justified. Although it’s set in the modern day, Justified is more of a traditional western than Deadwood was. Deadwood was brutal and bloody and painstakingly historically accurate, but Justified is a lighthearted action series about a U.S. Marshal chasing fugitives across the American frontier.

Justified was based on an Elmore Leonard short story, “Fire in the Hole,” and the dialogue has that Leonard-lite, Tarantinoan clip to it. Olyphant shares an endlessly riveting cat-and-mouse dynamic with his on-screen arch-nemesis Walton Goggins. In its first season, Justified started out as a traditional case-of-the-week show, but it became a more serialized drama starting in season 2, and then it was off to the races.

Vince Gilligan changed the way a story could be told on television with Breaking Bad. David Chase had introduced the notion of the TV antihero in The Sopranos, but Gilligan took it a step further and used the long-form medium of television to chronicle a character’s gradual turn from hero to villain. Tony Soprano is the same character in the pilot episode that he is in the series finale, but Walter White slowly transforms from a timid suburban family man into a ruthless drug lord with plenty of blood on his hands, and Bryan Cranston played every step of that dramatic journey pitch-perfectly.

Gilligan told that story of moral downfall through the lens of a contemporary western. He set Breaking Bad on the dusty streets and sun-scorched deserts of Albuquerque; he put an armed standoff in every other episode; he even had Walt and his cohorts rob a train in the final season.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of ScreenRant, directly in your inbox.

10 Hilarious Blondie Comics That Mock The Modern Day

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Since 1930, Blondie and Dagwood Bumstead have been navigating domestic life, delighting readers with jokes and comedic moments that have stood the test of time. Blondie’s success is no small feat: it is one of the longest-running and most influential comic strips of all time, even making a cameo in other popular strips like Garfield.

Blondie achieved remarkable longevity because it proved fertile ground for satirical strips that mock modern life. These strips contrast traditional mid-century sensibilities with today’s hyperconnected, high-speed reality. These iconic characters are placed in situations that hold a mirror up to readers and mock what has become commonplace in modern life.

This article highlights 10 Blondie comics that mock the modern day, showcasing the long-running series at its sharpest and funniest. By juxtaposing classic strip tropes with the chaotic landscape of smartphones, selfies and modern trends, Blondie has perfected the formula for comedy gold.

This comic strip satirises how obsessed people have become with their smartphones. Something as simple as reading a book has become a difficult task in an era when people’s attention spans have been greatly reduced by social media and fast-paced, easily accessible content.

The strip opens with Dagwood asking his daughter what book she is reading, and she replies, “A book for my book report that’s due tomorrow.” Dagwood, still thinking that’s a good thing, replies, “Well, better late than never, right?” but she comically reveals that she hasn’t started the book at all, showing her smartphone hidden among the books.

This joke pokes fun at how smartphones have made procrastination so easy. It is now easier than ever to get distracted or spend hours scrolling, even when deadlines need to be met. Many people would rather scroll for hours than spend an hour reading a book and doing their homework.

Technology has made many things easier, both at home and in the workplace, yet it still cannot load your dishes for you. This strip satirises how the rise of new technology has made people lazier than ever. Even something as simple as loading dishes into a dishwasher is something people wish an app could handle.

In the strip, Dagwood comments that “there’s literally an App for Everything”, and Blondie agrees and immediately chips in, saying, “ Too bad there isn’t an app for loading the Dishes”, and the next panel is Dagwood loading up the dishes and lamenting that “there should be an app for this”.

Beyond being a hilarious strip, it mocks how dependent people have become on technology, even for everyday tasks. It also offers an ironic commentary on how men avoid household chores, even though they expect their wives to do them.

Social media platforms such as Facebook are a good way to stay in touch with friends and family; however, the access they provide can often allow others to criticise or comment on things that involve other people’s personal lives. Additionally, it can lead to unintended consequences, like flirting, in an attempt to defend someone online.

The strip opens with Blondie commenting on how much time Dagwood has been spending on the “New neighbourhood Facebook page”, and Dagwood chips in about how much people have been complaining about Barbie Babewlder sunning herself in her bikini. Blondie makes a snarky comment about how he couldn’t help defending her, and Dagwood claims he can’t help being a peacemaker.

Unfortunately, the tables turn when Blondie sits down at the computer and says, “Speaking of sunning, Hank Hunkerman is cleaning his pool”. It catches Dagwood by surprise, and he then understands how Blondie felt earlier. He had unintentionally been crossing boundaries and flirting, but social media normalises it and makes it possible.

It is commonplace these days to make fun of young people for being addicted to social media, and not without merit. Using Alexander, Dagwood’s teenage son, this strip highlights how people are less likely to go outside for a walk because they are too busy scrolling on social media.

In the strip, Alexander complains that “reels” on Instagram and Facebook are driving him crazy. He then asks his Dad how he can escape all the reels on Social media, and Dagwood replies, “Have you tried ‘real life’?. Nobody is forced to scroll on social media against their will; some people just need a reminder to log off.

The world today is full of so many issues that some may find it insensitive to have silly, goofy, lighthearted days at work. Still, employers continue to find ways to break up the monotony of a 9-to-5 with fun workplace activities. The current sociopolitical climate may be concerning, but having some fun at work won’t make things any worse.

In this strip, Dagwood’s office decides to have a funny T-shirt day to commemorate the first day of summer, much to the dismay of one of his coworkers, who comments, “Really? In today’s world?” The office is filled with people wearing fun T-shirts, like the ironic “I love money” T-shirt Dagwood’s boss wears and a coworker’s Garfield T-shirt.

It’s pretty much a fact that Dagwood is lazy and would rather do anything else than chores. Nonetheless, this strip makes fun of how people often put on television to gain motivation to clean and do chores. It’s very common today to throw on something to watch in the background because people need that extra stimulation.

In this strip, Blondie tells Dagwood they have a lot of spring cleaning to do, since she has prepared an official cleaning list. Dagwood, trying to avoid chores, offers “the perfect way to get motivated”, which turns out to be a suggestion to watch an hour of HGTV and westerns, which Blondie soundly rejects.

In the modern age, information is easy to access, but misinformation is just as widespread. The internet makes it easy for misinformation to spread through unregulated forums and social media platforms that let people post whatever they like. Blondie satirises the spread of celebrity rumours by using iconic Simpsons characters.

The strip features Dagwood in bed with Blondie, using an iPad, and he complains about fake online rumours about a celebrity couple. Asked who, he says Homer and Marge Simpson, and Blondie remarks that it must be a slow news day. Readers can relate to clickbait titles and articles engineered to farm engagement.

In this strip, Blondie perfectly captures how corny it is when middle-aged people use Facebook and chat-speak. Today, it’s not uncommon for people to spend hours chatting with friends and family, even at work, often neglecting important and pressing duties.

In the strip, Dagwood is on his computer and sees that his friend Woodley has been on Facebook for an hour. Unaware of the irony that he is also goofing off on Facebook, he tells Woodley to get off Facebook and do his job. The two, along with Dwitsel Tweezer, then spend the next few panels goofing off and trash-talking.

This is interrupted when Dagwood’s boss walks into the room and asks whether he has finished the Heinwoofer Contract, and he replies, “Not yet, Boss! But I’ve been typing like a madman all morning!” In all fairness, he wasn’t technically lying; still, goofing off at work like this has become a common occurrence these days.

Blogging about your personal life online or posting life updates has become normal in today’s society, but Blondie shows readers how bizarre it really is. People detail their entire lives online, yet still expect a certain level of privacy, which can seem contradictory.

It begins with a panel showing Dagwood’s daughter sitting at the computer, and Dagwood walks up to ask what she is doing. She says she is blogging about her personal life online, and in the next panel she covers the screen with her hands and asks her father not to look, saying it is private.

Ordinarily, if you’re too ashamed to show your father something, you probably shouldn’t post it online, but these days it may be stranger not to. Social media keeps blurring the line between what should be public and what should be private, making the strip a hilarious piece of satire and irony.

The telephone has long evolved beyond what its inventor, Alexander Graham Bell, envisioned. Comparing the first phone with a modern smartphone makes the smartphone seem like pure wizardry. Blondie uses this premise to create a strip that mocks how people mainly use these devices to send cute cat videos and text jokes to their friends.

The first panel of the strip shows Dagwood conversing with a co-worker by a water dispenser, when the co-worker asks, “What would Alexander Graham Bell do if he saw how his invention has turned into this crazy smartphone Mania?” Dagwood sharply replied that he would send his friend Watson a cute cat video.

His co-worker ironically decided to text the joke to his buddy Ziggy. Not only does the strip explore how far phones have evolved, but it also comments on people’s obsession with and reliance on smartphones. The co-worker couldn’t help but text his buddy a joke he had just heard.

Boy Meets World’s Danielle Fishel Reveals One Topanga-Related Regret, And I Completely Understand

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A Full House Actor Had ‘Too Big A Pack’ To Be Wearing Biker Shorts In One Kimmy Gibbler Scene

Danica McKellar Wants You To Know She Didn’t LEAVE Hallmark. How She Feels

I Don’t Know How I Feel About ‘The Rachel’ Making A Comeback, But Jennifer Aniston Has Thoughts

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Danielle Fishel charmed audiences for seven seasons while playing the TGIF fan-favorite character, Topanga Lawrence in Boy Meets World. Not only was the character notable for being involved in a TV relationship that defined goals, but Topanga was also loved for being smart and true to herself. While Fishel has taken great pride in her BMW work, she recently mentioned one regret related to her character that, as a fan, I completely sympathize with.

At the beginning of the ABC sitcom, Topanga was introduced as Cory Matthews’ hippie-like classmate whose otherworldly views always sparked great comedic moments. But by the second season, th flower child had an extreme personality change, where she transitioned to an extremely motivated student and later the object of Cory’s affections by Season 3.

In Fishel, Rider Strong, and Will Friedle’s new book, Book Meets World: The Definitive Inside Story of the Hit Sitcom Boy Meets World (via People), she got real about her character's evolution. She explained how she felt about Topanga's trajectory at the time compared to how she feels now:

Teenage Danielle was more comfortable being the more mainstream Topanga because they wrote her more like me. So I probably didn’t have a problem with it then. Now I think, what a gift to be given a character that has such a sharply defined POV, to be so unique and to have been such a good role model. I knew that she was special. But then, when it changed, and she kind of evolved, I don’t think I necessarily knew to mourn it.

I get what the Dancing with the Stars contestant is talking about. When Fishel previously spoke about Topanga’s personality shift, she admitted that she didn’t even notice the change at the time. Looking back, it’s true that the show was focusing a lot on Topanga’s evolving look, like in Season 2 when Shawn was staring at her chest or Cory fixated so much on her beauty in Season 3. I truly believe Topanga’s complete change was when she cut her trademark long hair and started to look more like many other woman of the ‘90s.

While no one formally told the Dish host that her character would deviate from her weird girl persona, she said she remembered hearing a conversation from the crew about wanting to make Topanga “more normal” for Cory. Yet, even in the Season 6 episode, "Resurrection," Cory talked about missing the old Topanga. I feel like Cory would have grown to like the more off-beat version of his eventual partner.

Something that's interesting is hearing Fishel mention that as a teen, she didn't really mind her character being more streamlined and fitting her own personality. She may not have mourned the transition then, but she certainly does now:

Now I see it, and I so mourn it. I would have loved even little flashbacks to it.

That would have been great. Fans see a flashback scene in “Resurrection” of when Topanga put lipstick all over her face but, in the later seasons, I would have liked to have seen Fishel's character retain traits of who she was early on.

At the same time, we can be thankful that no matter who Topanga grew up to be, she remained throughout the series in general. Some may not be aware that she was originally written to be a recurring character. As a result of her sticking around, fans got to see her transform into a confident young woman, who even became an incredible lawyer, wife, and mother in the spinoff series, Girl Meets World.

While Danielle Fishel now regrets having leaned into Topanga’s personality change, I'd argue that her performance on the show still deserves praise. And, of course, we can love the iconic character for who she was when she was first introduced as well as who she grew into by the show’s series finale. You can watch Topanga’s Boy Meets World and Girl Meets World journeys by streaming the sitcoms with a Disney+ subscription.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

Regis: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Regis's guest traffic fell 1.7% while franchisees closed 34 salons, leaving the turnaround's success tied to whether it can stop that erosion.

On its first quarter of fiscal 2027 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Regis (RGS -1.38%) detailed its plan to boost guest traffic and stabilize franchisee operations. The salon franchisor is navigating customer traffic declines while managing debt reduction and store closures. Three things management said stand out.

Supercuts system traffic fell 1.7% during the quarter, making guest acquisition and retention a central priority. Management is expanding Choice Booking, an online scheduling tool that allows digital appointments alongside traditional walk-ins, after test locations saw higher customer visits and fewer online booking errors.

Management evaluated multiple refinancing options for its $121.1 million outstanding debt, including bank loans and private credit financing. Potential lenders requested additional quarters of operational progress before agreeing to new terms, prompting the company to delay refinancing until its prepayment penalty drops in June 2027.

Franchisees closed 34 locations in the first quarter, threatening recurring fee streams. To mitigate revenue erosion, management launched an artificial intelligence (AI) health dashboard to identify underperforming locations early and facilitate salon transfers to stronger operators.

Operational turnarounds are underway across core salon brands while the company maintains positive cash flow. Management is focusing on franchise resales, localized marketing, and expanded digital booking tools to stabilize system revenue.

Long-term outcomes depend on whether operational changes can curb guest traffic erosion and reduce salon closures. Aligning franchisee execution with modernized tools remains critical to building salon-level profitability.

Cars.com: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Self-service listings target smaller dealers and budget inventory, but the article leaves open whether that growth can coexist with the margin gains.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Cars.com (CARS -1.09%) delivered record quarterly adjusted EBITDA, a measure of core operational profitability, despite flat top-line growth. Long-term investors should note how cost discipline and new digital tools are expanding operating leverage while filling inventory gaps. Three things management said stand out.

Management introduced a no-fee starter package that lets smaller dealers list vehicles without manual onboarding assistance. The initiative expands platform inventory in the under-$20,000 price range, attracting budget-conscious shoppers and creating future upsell opportunities.

Organizational changes made earlier in the year removed management layers and reduced duplicated work across business units. These cost-containment efforts helped push the adjusted EBITDA margin, which measures profitability before interest, tax, and accounting charges, to nearly 31%.

The Carson artificial intelligence shopping assistant reached over 1 million monthly users as conversational search behavior expanded on the platform. Management shifted marketing spend toward high-intent shoppers, driving roughly double-digit growth in sales leads despite lower overall site traffic.

Operating leverage is expanding across the core marketplace as organizational restructuring takes hold. The business relies on high customer retention, direct integrations with artificial intelligence traffic, and broad dealer coverage to maintain its market footprint while monitoring pricing discipline and subscription adoption rates across its platform.

Management plans to extend its operational framework to underperforming website solutions while maintaining share repurchases.

11 gadgets under $10 that solve everyday problems

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You don't have to spend much to fix little annoyances in everyday life.

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

Steve Jobs once said that customers don't know what they want until you show them. And he was right. But he was also lucky because he was showing people iPhones and not, say, rechargeable nose trimmers.

Of course, when Apple launched the iPhone in 2007, Amazon wasn't the global behemoth it's since become. Now there's virtually nothing Amazon doesn't sell, and a lot of it is dirt cheap. In the post-Bezos world, Jobsian marketing principles no longer apply. Customers still may not know what they want, but if it's cheap enough, they'll buy it anyway and then throw it out and buy something else.

Thus begins our quest to assemble a list of cheap gadgets that are worth every penny, which should come in handy around the shopping season. If you treat them right, these may last you for many holidays to come.

It's the middle of the night when you wake up and need to head to the en-suite bathroom. You snap on the light and you're blinded because it's too bright. Or worse, you wake up your spouse, who can't easily get back to sleep even with their favorite sleeping app.

This scenario may sound exaggerated, but if you've ever been there, you know it isn't. Or, to spin it back to Steve Jobs, you just don't realize what you need. In this case, it's a motion sensor light for your bathroom.

Decent motion-activated lights for around $10 that don't require any drilling come in two varieties. They're either battery-powered stick-ons like the Star-Spangled LED Puck Light ($8.99 for 5), or they plug right into a socket. The latter are more like smart night lights. The BN-Link Backlit Motion Sensor ($12.34 for 2), for example, has an auto mode that only activates with motion, plus a night mode that gives a dim light until activated.

In both cases, you get to see at night without waking anybody else up. And you're good to go for years because the bulbs are LEDs and use very little energy.

Those of us driving around without built-in infotainment displays in our cars have tried every so-called solution for mounting our phones: suction cups, clips, vent mounts and more. The more expensive and heartier the holder, the faster they wilt, with one exception: peel-off phone magnets.

This WixGear offering costs $6.99 and is simple to use. First, peel off the film on the back. Stick one on the back of your phone (or preferably your phone case), stick the other on your dashboard, and bring your phone close to snap them together. It shouldn't plop to the ground ever again.

A few caveats here. One, the stickiness of the adhesive makes this a permanent fix, so don't expect to move the magnets around once they're on. Two, we recommend finding a vertical position on your dashboard that's close enough to tap, but not too close to a vent. Extreme temperature changes might lead to your phone slipping, while heat blowing on it is never good.

And three, a phone stuck to your dashboard can easily become dangerous. Don't spend too much time playing with it; you could easily get distracted. Apple has also reported that magnets can influence photo quality in certain cases, so keep an eye on that. All said, for speaker phone calls, Google Maps or playing music in your car, peel-off magnets are a tested fix.

There are two types of people: those who are aware of keyboard gunk and those who have blissfully never observed it. If you don't make a habit of cleaning your keyboard, the first tool you're likely to reach for to remove gunk is a brush. You may, with much practice, reach a state of competency where you can finagle stray brush hairs into the cracks around individual keys and dislodge the detritus of your typing life that way. It works, but you can do better.

A handheld, mouse-sized vacuum cleaner may strike you as effete, and in a sense, it is. But for those who like a clean keyboard, a vacuum is quicker and much more efficient than hunting for crumbs with a brush.

Our recommendation also comes with a nozzle, brush and USB charging cable, amenities that nudge it a bit over the $10 mark. While you can find cheaper options, caveat emptor. Some of them look good but get horrible customer reviews. For best results, disconnect your keyboard from a desktop PC (or shut down your laptop) before you clean.

If you haven't kept up with other forms of computer cleaning, it's also worth learning how to clean your screen the right way.

Yes, it's just a lowly phone stand. But it could be a life-saver. If you doubt us, you've probably never had a last-minute Zoom interview where, in lieu of propping your iPhone up against a fruit bowl, you opted for a grainy, handheld shot that gave your potential employer a bold view of your inner nostrils. That isn't the only reason to invest in an inexpensive, versatile phone holder, however.

Modern versions of iOS and Android can turn your phone into a webcam, which is a great way to reuse an old smartphone. But unless you own a folding phone, you're back at square one.

A budget stand like the Nulaxy solves this problem neatly. Whatever stand you end up with, make sure it's universal so you don't have to buy one for everyone in the house. It should have a gap at the bottom to make room for the charging cable, and remain steady whether in landscape or portrait.

There is perhaps nothing more disturbing than a wax-bejeweled earbud. Actually, there is: having to send your dirty, malfunctioning bud back to wherever you purchased it for a diagnosis of "ear wax accumulation." The minds behind the $6.99 Hyashee earbud cleaning pen are on our wavelength here.

Hyashee's earbud cleaners are geared toward AirPods, though the flocking sponge and brush combo will get the job done for any other brand too. As an alternative, there are other universal options like the Midukit cleaner kit. At $9.99, this comes with everything from the Hyashee Cleaner Kit, plus blobs of purple putty and some brushes. Dirt sticks to the putty so you can get it out that way.

Investing a few dollars in an earbud cleaner could improve the longevity of your buds, which makes a difference if you own a quality pair of AirPods. It could also save you from the disgrace of having to send your grimy headphones to a horrified technician at Best Buy. This applies even more if you've got kids with earbuds of their own.

There always ends up being someone who appears less often in family photos because they're the one behind the camera. To restore balance to your family, consider a remote Bluetooth camera shutter. The ATUMTEK Wireless Camera Remote Shutter is a popular choice; it's $8.99 and comes as a two-pack. It should pair with your phone up to distances of ~30 feet, and is compatible with both Android and iOS.

The Zttopo Wireless Bluetooth Camera Shutter Remote is about the same price. Reviews for both hover around 4.5 stars on Amazon. Just be warned that you'll need a tripod (or at least a stand, as above) before you assemble your first full family portrait.

You might not even need to buy a new flash drive to make this work; rifle around in your desk drawer and if you get lucky, you'll unearth a dust-covered treasure from 2011. If you can't find one — or if this is a stocking stuffer for techy friends or relatives — you can always order something like a basic Sandisk 32GB USB 2.0 drive around $9.99.

Why would you want to turn a USB stick into a portable antivirus? Because it might be all you need to clean up an infected Windows machine without having to call the Geek Squad. There are plenty of other neat ways to use an old USB thumb drive aside from storage, but this is our favorite.

When an attack strikes, it can take over your OS, locking you out of your antivirus. When you boot up an antivirus from a separate disk (your USB drive), you can bypass the infected system and clean it from the outside. ESET SysRescue Live is one example that's bootable from a flash drive.

For many of us, the main duty of our Google Nest Hub or similar device is to serve as a bark-activated kitchen timer. "Hey Google, set a timer for 20 minutes!... Stop!"

Considering the expense, why not just buy a digital kitchen timer? They're a lot cheaper and you won't have to bark at Google ever again. This minimal DALSHABET timer kind of looks like a Google Nest Thermostat, which is to say, it looks pretty good. It's also magnetic, so it sticks to your refrigerator or any other magnetic surface in the kitchen. And it's cheap at $9.99, though it could drop a little depending on sales.

The only part you might have to get used to is setting it manually. But that's just a twist of the dial. Is this extra manual labor worth it? If you want to save money and avoid adding yet another sporadically obedient screen of dubious utility to your home, this little $10 dial should last you a while.

As with many aspects of digital hygiene, there are competing views on webcam covers, as well as various methods for physically blocking access to your computer's camera.

Former FBI director James Comey admitted to taping over his laptop's camera. Meta CEO and former human Mark Zuckerberg was photographed doing the same in 2016. If you're worried that your camera might accidentally pop on when you don't intend it to, a set of $8 BLOCKED vinyl webcam stickers might be useful. Keep in mind that an attack's goal is more likely to install malware or trick you into handing over credentials than to spy on you.

If you're on a Mac, the camera will never turn on without its accompanying green indicator light. You may also damage your screen if you slap on a traditional slide cover, since razor-thin laptop design doesn't allow room for them. That said, for work laptops or desktops you've had for a while, a vinyl sticker is low-cost protection against suddenly appearing at a team meeting in your pajamas.

If we ever calculated how much time we've lost, as a species, to USB-related inconveniences, we would drag USB's creator before the ICC. His name is Ajay Bhatt, by the way, and he's been hiding in broad daylight reaping the fruits of his evil genius since 1996.

Yes, it's just a cable. But oh, what havoc USB can wreak. The problems are endless, but the source of our grief is usually the same: we have the wrong connector for a device. Our machine could also feature the wrong port for the cable we're trying to use. Or, if we're on an Apple laptop, we probably don't have enough ports. In all three cases, we're stuck.

The usual solution is to buy an adapter, but that can still run into issues. Maybe the adapter only works for one situation, like USB-A to USB-C. Or we shell out way more for a multi-purpose hub that has ports handling a bunch of different connections, including HDMI. Fortunately, there's a middle route that costs a lot less: a USB adapter kit.

This 12-piece IANFAN USB kit, at $8.59, offers a bunch of adapters that should cover any USB compatibility issues you face. If you want a handier solution geared to USB-C compatibility, try the Povdite charging cable adapter kit, which comes with a case and its own charging cable. Whichever you choose, you'll be doing your part in the fight against uncontrolled cable sprawl.

Wireless charging pads present an interesting dilemma for the sustainability-conscious gadget shopper. Will replacing one accessory (a USB cable) with another (a charging pad) actually make your life easier and the planet better? Or will you just feed the yawning maw of your accessory drawer one more tasty meal?

A charging pad still needs to be powered by a cord, so it's not truly wireless. But once you plug it in, you won't have to worry about USB compatibility anymore, as long as your devices all support wireless charging. If you can find a quality charging pad that powers multiple devices, that's even better. But that can get tricky, especially when your budget is 10 dollars.

Cheaper charging pads also might not charge as quickly, as outputs vary. And one station might not be enough for a whole household. That said, if you want to test the waters, this NANAMI Fast Wireless Charger Pad costs $9.99. It works with Android phones, iPhones and even earbuds, with a maximum charging power of 15W.

I Just Found Out Why Disneyland Doesn’t Do Fake Snow During Rainstorms (Because Eww)

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If You’re Looking For A Christmas Movie Outside Of Netflix And Hallmark, Hulu Has You Covered This Week

Netflix’s A Dog’s Perfect Christmas Made Me Cry, But Not For The Reasons I Thought

Upcoming Hallmark Movies: Full List Of New Premiere Dates, Including The 2026 Countdown To Christmas

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Don't let it snow, let it snow, let it snow!

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

As a native Southern Californian, I’ve never seen a white Christmas. The closest I can get without getting on a plane is during Disneyland’s holiday season. Every night, without fail, Main Street U.S.A. and the area in front of “It’s A Small World” become a winter wonderland, and it feels like a fantasy. That is, unless it's raining. I didn’t know before exactly why rain would stop the snow from falling upon Disneyland, but someone just made the reason quite evident.

“Disneyland’s Wintertime Enchantment” makes snowfall in Southern California possible every night at 7:30 p.m. and 8:30 p.m. However, don’t count on it if it’s raining. And FYI, there’s a lot of it coming this week after some notable flooding at Disneyland last month. Here’s why:

A post shared by Molly Evans (@happilyevansafterall)

Now, I hate to ruin the magic, but Disneyland snow isn’t actually snow. It’s something that’s been named "snoap" by fans, because it’s practically soap disguised as snow. It's dispersed by hidden rooftop machines.

According to the Los Angeles Times, Disneyland’s snow is made up of 98% water mixed with a non-hazardous, proprietary surfactant (soap) blend. On any other day, the “snow” evaporates shortly after landing somewhere and leaves no trace on guests or around the park. However, when too much water mixes with it, you get a very soapy (and gross) ground!

As you can see in the Instagram post, when the “snoap” and rain happen at the same time, it creates a major slip hazard for the guests. Therefore, it is usually canceled on rainy days. In this recent instance, the guest reports that snow was accidentally turned on at 10:30 p.m., causing an issue everywhere in the park where snow fell.

Even if the snowfall is stalled by rain, most of the other holiday festivities can still happen during such weather conditions. The firework show will have to be canceled during active rain, heavy cloud cover or high winds, but the projections at “It’s A Small World” will typically still run.

The “It’s A Small World” ride also has a special holiday version if you’re not into that song. The World of Color - Season of Light over at California Adventure can also usually hold up unless rain is paired with severe wind or lightning. And you can meet Santa Claus at Mickey’s Toontown Trolley Barn, which is located indoors.

One of my personal favorite holiday traditions is the A Christmas Fantasy Parade. I just love seeing all the characters in their Christmas-themed outfits, too. Over at DCA, there’s also a holiday event called “Guardians of the Galaxy: Awesome Dance Off!” debuting at Avengers Campus. Disneyland’s holiday-themed entertainment runs until January 6 this year, so there’s plenty of time to enjoy this special time of year.

If you’re not able to make it to Disneyland right now, it’s also a good time to check out the 2026 Christmas movie schedule. A ton are available to stream with a Disney+ subscription!

‘Toy Story 5’ Producer Reflects On ‘Level Of Complexity’ In Technology That Film Franchise Has Grown To Match With Curly Hair Tool Written For Blaze – Contenders L.A.

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As evidenced by the latest Toy Story film, technology is here to stay in toys and beyond.

For producer Lindsey Collins, whose credits also include Disney/Pixar‘s A Bug’s Life, Ratatouille, Finding Nemo and Turning Red, the key is balancing not overdeveloping the looks of animations in the films with moving them forward to suit advancements humanity has made and better capture certain details.

“[We] try to keep it so we’re not evolving it so fast or so much that people are like, ‘This doesn’t feel like the Toy Story I recognize or I know,’ but also make sure that we’re keeping it so that we’re taking advantage of all the things we can do,” Collins said on a panel at Deadline’s Contenders Film Los Angeles event. “The technology just lets us do anything now. At the time, it was so restricted, like no hair or fur on the dog, or you couldn’t have you have tight clothing on everybody. One of the things that we’re really proud of is the character Blaze, who we all love, who’s the friend that ultimately comes as the gift at the end of the film. Curly hair is really hard.

RELATED: ‘Toy Story 5’ Writer-Director Andrew Stanton Wants To Bring Nuance To The Conversation Between Kids & Technology Watch on Deadline

“We’ve done curly hair before,” she added. “We’ve done it on Brave, but it is a technology that’s really hard to make look right. So we wrote a whole tool for the first time on this film to really capture the beauty and the gorgeousness of Blaze’s hair, and it took a ton of consultants internally to make sure: ‘Does it feel right? Does that look right? Does that feel like it looks natural to her?’ Those are the kinds of things that we try to do to take advantage of all this technology now to go, ‘Oh my gosh, we can now actually start to deliver a real sense of texture and softness,’ and that’s the kind of stuff that we would have cheated on all the other films.”

Collins also marveled at the ability to create 50 Buzz Lightyears on 50 horses in the fifth film versus the possibility of rendering one in the 1999 original.

RELATED: The 65 Movies That Have Made More Than $1 Billion At The Global Box Office

“The level of complexity [of things] we can now do is pretty amazing,” she said.

There was a version of the film’s ending where star character Jessie (voiced by Joan Cusack) meets an older version of her previous owner, Emily.

“Because it was Jessie’s story, we knew we wanted her to reckon with the past with Emily, and there was a version at one point where she actually meets Emily, all grown up and old, and and we were like, “That’s a trip. OK, does that feel satisfying?’ As an audience, there was a lot of conversation around what’s going to be the satisfying gift for Jesse at the end,” Collins said. “Is it seeing Emily? Is it kind of understanding, getting the chance to kind of play with her again? These toys are like vampires; they don’t age. The rest of the world ages around them. We knew it had to be something that felt like it was the gift for her that she needed, the closure, and so that last scene with the buried treasure under the tree was the thing that probably took the longest to land.”

RELATED: The 30 Highest-Grossing Animated Films Of All Time At The Global Box Office

Collins closed the panel with reluctance that the films could eventually center AI, but she also didn’t rule out a next film to complete the “Bonnie years” trilogy just as Andy got three films.

Netflix's New Firefly Replacement Series Can Be Binged In Less Than 4 Hours

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

While it has been a long time since Firefly graced the airwaves, Netflix’s new space western is a four-hour binge that offers an ideal alternative to the iconic series. There are few one-season TV shows that have quite as long a half life as the short-lived space western Firefly. Firefly’s 14-episode run was met with mixed reviews and underwhelming ratings, but that wasn’t the end of the space opera’s story.

Firefly’s superb DVD sales, as well as the outspoken fan community online, prompted a movie spinoff in the form of 2005’s Serenity. Even though Serenity wasn’t a box office hit, the movie was beloved by critics, and a massive array of subsequent sci-fi shows acknowledged Firefly as a formative influence. There is SyFy’s space opera Killjoys, which often feels like the show Firefly could have been, as well as 2015’s Dark Matter, which was a love letter to Firefly in tone and premise alike.

In 2016, Jonathan Nolan’s Westworld reboot took Firefly’s mix of a futuristic sci-fi setting and traditional western elements and turned these same ingredients into a darker, more austere dystopian mystery box series. More recently, Netflix’s latest high-profile show has blended western and sci-fi tropes in a fresh, original story that has more than a touch of Firefly to it. An animated show created by LeSean Thomas, Bass X Machina takes place in a steampunk wild west where supernatural threats, giant robots, and gunslinging cowboys vie for dominance.

Within this pitiless milieu, Brian Tyree Henry voices the titular Bass Reeves, a bounty hunter and Civil War veteran. Joining Bass is Tati Gabrille’s Dana, a teenage engineer searching for her uncle who has disappeared, and Cree Summer’s Ahni, a magical mystic. With a superb ensemble voice cast including Wayne Knight and Janelle Monáe, Bass X Machina has no shortage of star power.

However, much like Apple TV’s Murderbot, this sci-fi series isn’t reliant on its impressive cast alone to keep viewers engaged. With epic, immersive battles, constant twists, and a wild mix of genres at its core, Bass X Machina is a truly fresh take on the real-life story of Bass Reeves that is unpredictable, inventive, and brimming with the unlikely fusion of sci-fi and western plot elements that made Firefly so memorable all those years ago.

On the big screen, attempts to mix sci-fi and westerns have often fallen flat. One need only look at expensive misfires like 2011’s Cowboys & Aliens and 1999’s Wild Wild West to know that, for whatever reason, the pairing doesn’t seem to attract interest from audiences at the box office. Moreover, even the kindest critics would struggle to call these movies overlooked classics.

Westerns and sci-fi don’t seem to mix at a practical level when it comes to feature films, at least not on a blockbuster scale. In contrast, Bass X Machina proves that Netflix’s animated series could take the same character who was the subject of a po-faced Taylor Sheridan Western series, Lawman: Bass Reeves, and have him fighting killer machines alongside paranormal mystics in a twisted reinvention of the Wild West.

With stunning imagery that calls to mind Netflix’s earlier genre blender Blue Eye Samurai, Bass X Machina admittedly makes a pretty dramatic visual contrast with Firefly’s deliberately low-tech vision of the far future. However, fans of Firefly’s fast-paced action, sharp dialogue, themes of found family, and truly original mix of the two genres will find plenty to love here.

Only time will tell if Bass X Machina will receive a second season, or if the show will suffer the same ignominious fate as Firefly as well as sharing its atypical mix of genres. However, for now, Netflix’s Bass X Machina is an ideal substitute for any Firefly fans who still miss the influential show.

Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 14 novembre

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

La Juventus guarda anche a Gustavo Varela per rinforzare il proprio reparto offensivo. L'attaccante portoghese del Monza, attuale capocannoniere della Serie A con 9 reti, è finito nel mirino dei bianconeri, alla ricerca di un centravanti da mettere a disposizione di Luciano Spalletti già nel mercato di gennaio. Come riferisce il Corriere dello Sport, Varela rappresenta una possibile alternativa qualora la dirigenza juventina non riuscisse a concretizzare un'operazione per un attaccante di maggiore esperienza. Il suo rendimento ha attirato l'attenzione di numerose società, rendendo però particolarmente complicata un'eventuale trattativa. Si tratta di Napoli in Serie A, ma anche club esteri come Barcellona, Borussia Dortmund e Lipsia.

Como attento ai talenti che si mettono in mostra in giro per il mondo. Uno di questi è Hamza Abdelkarim, punta classe 2008 di nazionalità egiziana, legato al Barcellona da un contratto valido fino al 30 giugno 2030. Il giocatore starebbe valutando di lasciare il club blaugrana dopo aver faticato a ritagliarsi uno spazio in prima squadra (appena 12 minuti tra tutte le competizioni) sotto la guida di Hansi Flick. Cesc Fabregas, al contrario, potrebbe garantirgli un impiego superiore con due soli attaccanti in rosa (Douvikas e Kean), mentre dal canto suo Abdelkarim non sarebbe così propenso a rimanere nella cantera del Barça a 18 anni.

Il PSV Eindhoven insiste per Kevin De Bruyne, ma il Napoli non ha intenzione, almeno per il momento, di privarsi del centrocampista belga. Il club olandese vorrebbe provare a convincere l'ex Manchester City a trasferirsi già a gennaio, anche se l'operazione si presenta complicata. Come riferisce il Corriere dello Sport, la posizione della società azzurra è chiara: De Bruyne resta un elemento importante per Massimiliano Allegri, che vorrebbe continuare ad averlo a disposizione nella seconda parte della stagione.

"Possono pagarti quanto vogliono, ma lo stress è tuo, del tuo corpo”. Parlava così Stanislav Lobotka prima dell'ultimo infortunio muscolare che lo ha fatto fermare per sei settimane. Possibile un suo rientro intorno a metà dicembre, ma nel frattempo il Napoli deve fare di necessità virtù, utilizzando Gilmour come schermo davanti alla difesa. Non è la stessa cosa, ovviamente. Lo slovacco percepiva 2,5 milioni di euro all'inizio della sua avventura a Napoli, mentre nel 2024 ha contrattato uno stipendio variabile verso l'alto fino a 4,5 milioni. Una cifra che per il club di De Laurentiis incomincia a essere troppo alta, al netto del Decreto Crescita: Lobotka difficilmente sarà una plusvalenza ed è possibile che vada via a parametro zero, nell'ottica di rifondare e cambiare la filosofia contrattuale. Sulla falsariga di quello che succederà ad Anguissa, insomma, la sensazione è che sia l'ultima stagione per Lobotka a Napoli.

Attenzione al futuro di Hakan Calhanoglu. Il centrocampista turco è a scadenza di contratto con l'Inter e come normale che sia la sua situazione inizia ad attrarre gli occhi indiscreti di chi lo prenderebbe volentieri con sé a parametro zero. In particolare negli ultimi giorni si è discusso dell'interesse della Juventus, oltre che di quello del Como. Al momento però c'è un ma importante dato che il calciatore, almeno fino a questo momento, dà priorità all'Inter. Il club avrebbe poi promesso al giocatore e ai suoi agenti di voler parlare di rinnovo attraverso una formula con più bonus e uno stipendio fisso un po' più basso. Bisogna però poi trovare gli accordi poiché Calhanoglu ha la testa all'Inter e i dirigenti gli hanno promesso di sedersi a un tavolo per trovare una soluzione. Si vedrà dunque come proseguirà la situazione.

Luis Henrique è tornato ad allenarsi in gruppo e sarà a disposizione di Cristian Chivu per la trasferta contro l'Atalanta. Il recupero dall'infortunio, tuttavia, non sembra destinato a cambiare le gerarchie sulla fascia nerazzurra. Secondo Tuttosport, l'ex Marsiglia rischia di restare ai margini anche nelle prossime settimane, complice la crescita dei concorrenti. Djed Spence si è ormai imposto sulla destra, mentre Andy Diouf rappresenta un'alternativa preziosa anche a gara in corso. Sull'altra corsia, Federico Dimarco sta ritrovando continuità e rendimento, con Carlos Augusto che ha sfruttato le opportunità ricevute, dimostrando di poter incidere sia da esterno sia da braccetto. Una concorrenza che ha progressivamente ridotto gli spazi per il brasiliano. Un impiego limitato che potrebbe spingere il giocatore a valutare nuove opportunità già nella finestra invernale. In estate la Roma aveva mostrato interesse, mentre in precedenza il nome dell'esterno era finito anche sul taccuino di Tiago Pinto per il Bournemouth. Il brasiliano, tuttavia, non aveva manifestato particolare entusiasmo per la destinazione giallorossa e aveva respinto gli approcci dalla Turchia, preferendo attendere eventuali proposte dalla Premier League. Lo scarso minutaggio a Milano potrebbe ora portarlo a rivedere le proprie priorità.

La Roma lavora per rinforzare la rosa in vista della sessione di mercato e cerca quel trequartista sinistro di piede destro che Gian Piero Gasperini chiede fin dal suo arrivo nella Capitale. Tra i diversi tavoli aperti dal ds Tony D'Amico, uno dei principali porta a Londra, sponda Chelsea, per Jamie Gittens. Il Chelsea potrebbe aprire a una cessione in prestito con diritto o obbligo di riscatto, sebbene a Stamford Bridge credano ancora nelle sue qualità mostrate in passato con il Borussia Dortmund. La Roma valuta attentamente le condizioni fisiche del giocatore, chiuso nella rosa inglese da profili come Neto, Estevao, Quenda e Rogers, ma resta fortemente interessata a riportarlo al centro del progetto tecnico di Gasperini per rinforzare la corsia offensiva sinistra nella seconda parte della stagione calcistica.

In casa Napoli, qualcosa potrebbe accadere per gli esterni visto che Lang è in uscita (anche se per il momento non sono arrivate offerte) ma qualche dubbio c’è anche su Neres, su cui c’è molto interesse in Brasile. E allora ecco che in questo caso ci sarebbe modo di inserire un giocatore con quel tipo di caratteristiche. Il profilo che piace di più è sicuramente Cacciamani già cercato anche dalla Roma in estate. Operazione davvero difficile anche perché il club granata non ha certo intenzione di cederlo a metà stagione. Un’altra soluzione è Gittens, che il Chelsea potrebbe cedere in prestito o magari anche a condizioni vantaggiose. Insomma il Napoli è al lavoro, a breve partiranno le grandi manovre.

Il primo posto in classifica non può che far sognare. Nessuno avrebbe mai immaginato che la Lazio dopo undici giornate fosse al comando della Serie A con 29 punti, frutto di ben 9 vittorie e due pareggi. Unica squadra ancora imbattuta. Una soddisfazione enorme, che adesso porta a nuove considerazioni anche sul mercato, sempre che a gennaio si verifichi la possibilità. I biancocelesti devono infatti ancora capire come potranno muoversi ma intanto c’è una certezza: tutti i big resteranno. Per cui chi volesse avvicinarsi ai vari Taylor, Nuno Tavares, Isaksen e tutti gli altri può tranquillamente mettersi l’animo in pace. Insomma nessuna cessione e magari qualche pensiero per un eventuale inserimento. Al momento l’idea Icardi rimane tale, nel senso che non ci sono mai stati veramente passi concreti ma solo sondaggi senza mai entrare nello specifico. Questo però non significa che il presidente Lotito non abbia in mente di rafforzare la squadra magari con un altro esterno offensivo. Un giocatore che piace è sicuramente Dennis Man del PSV Ehindoven, con un passato al Parma a cui non dispiacerebbe la possibilità di tornare a giocare in Serie A.

Due assist e un gol per Donyell Malen, addirittura tripletta per Tijjani Reijnders. L'Olanda di Xavi si è divertita a ridicolizzare la Grecia 7-0 ieri nel confronto valido il quinto turno di UEFA Nations League, salendo a quota 11 punti con vista sulla qualificazione ai quarti di finale - anche se tutto dipenderà dall'incrocio finale con la Germania di Klopp. Ma davvero rimarrà all'Al Qadsiah? I numeri in Saudi Pro League sono da top player, vice capocannoniere con ben 8 gol alla sua prima esperienza lontano dall'Europa e in Medio Oriente. Anche davanti all'ex Atalanta e oggi compagno di squadra Mateo Retegui.

Il nome di Georges Mikautadze è risuonato con forza nella fase finale del calciomercato in vista di un possibile arrivo al Barcellona, quando la dirigenza catalana continuava a non riuscire a ingaggiare Julian Alvarez. Il georgiano di proprietà del Villarreal - con tanto di contratto duraturo fino al 2031 - non è finito dove avrebbe voluto, ma ciò non ha intaccato il grande affetto che continua a provare per i colori blaugrana. Lo ha affermato l’attaccante in un’intervista a DAZN. Mikautadze, che a poco a poco si sta facendo un nome sempre più importante al Villarreal, è stato interrogato sull’interesse mostrato dal Barça durante l’estate e il calciatore non ha esitato a mostrare il suo grande affetto per la squadra guidata da Hansi Flick. "È lusinghiero. Il Barça è il club del mio cuore, lo guardo giocare da quando ero piccolo. Continuo a seguire le sue partite anche adesso", ha commentato l’attaccante 26enne, autore di 5 gol in questa stagione, di cui tre in Liga e altri due in Champions League.

Nel mondo esiste un altro Ronal­di­nho? A rispondere alla domanda ci penso direttamente il fuoriclasse brasiliano, che sta regalando emozioni in queste ora ai tifosi del Ravenna e più in generale a tutti gli appassionati di calcio che potranno rivedere in campo l'ex campione di Barcellona e Milan domani contro il Forlì: "Non mi piace fare para­goni - ha sottolineato lo stesso Ronaldinho al Corriere della Sera -. Ci sono tanti gio­ca­tori che mi piac­ciono, i bra­si­liani per esem­pio". E a proposito di brasiliani, inevitabile parlare di Endrick, attaccante del Real Madrid che piace per gennaio a Milan e Roma: "Se è pronto per la Serie A? Può gio­care in ogni cam­pio­nato", sottolinea Ronaldinho che poi a La Gazzetta dello Sport aggiunge: "È un ragazzo che può giocare in tutte le parti del mondo: ha fatto tanti campionati e bene, in Francia, Brasile e Spagna, è molto giovane e può giocare facendo bene sì...".

Il Benfica blinda Gianluca Prestianni. Manuel Rui Costa ha confermato infatti che il club portoghese è ormai vicino all'accordo per il rinnovo del giovane attaccante argentino, già autore di 9 gol e 6 assist in 16 presenze ufficiali con gli Encarnados quest'anno. Il presidente delle Aquile ha parlato del futuro del classe 2006 a margine dell'inaugurazione della 256ª Casa do Benfica a Cantanhede, dove ha risposto alle domande dei giornalisti sui principali temi di attualità della società. "È praticamente tutto pronto per il rinnovo e andrà in porto senza alcun problema. Firmerà a breve, è tutto già sistemato", ha dichiarato Rui Costa, confermando che le trattative sono ormai alle battute finali.
